# Activity Centre — surface brief

## Scope and mode

The Activity Centre wing: the home list (`/activities`), each Activity's intro, and the eight worlds that fill the runner's left column. Mode: **Experience** for the worlds (the student is inside the room the skill lives in), **Operate** for the answer loop on the right, which stays on the module's cream surface untouched.

## Direction (locked 2026-08-21)

**Eight worlds, eight universes.** Every Activity opens into a fully committed physical scene, saturated and material, scoped behind its wrapper class so nothing leaks into the shell. This is a deliberate, owner-approved departure from the app-wide monochrome-editorial restraint: the Centre is the one wing where the room IS the teaching instrument. The Coach and answer loop remain the constant; the room changes.

| World | Activity | Scene | Display face | Key hues |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | obsidian void split by an aurora; survey-grid floor; prismatic glass plate | Italiana | ultraviolet 275°, polar cyan 190° |
| Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | Victorian naturalist's study; green wall, parchment bench, bell jar on brass plinth | EB Garamond | botanical green 162°, brass 40°, madder 8° |
| Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | celestial cartographer's dome; nebulae, twinkling stars, gilt ribs | Marcellus | indigo 233°, gold 45°, comet cyan 187°, rose 330° |
| Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | midnight rail yard; sodium floods, fog, converging track fan, solari board | Barlow Condensed | steel-night 213°, sodium 36°, signal emerald 158° |
| Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | stained-glass workshop; lamp cone, dust, tinted panes in lead cames | Young Serif | umber 24°, lamp gold 40°, glass tints rotating |
| Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | torchlit porphyry rotunda; bronze protractor inlaid in the floor | Cinzel | porphyry 356°, torchlight 36°, bronze 30° |
| The Press | Dumb it Down | hot-metal print shop; ink rollers, iron frame, vermilion proof red | Bevan | iron 20°, paper cream 42°, vermilion 14° |
| The Loom | Spot the Callback | indigo dye-house; warp threads, linen sheets with selvage, saffron thread | Alegreya | indigo 236°, linen 43°, saffron 42° |

Statement/body text across dark worlds runs Spectral 300; micro-labels stay on the shared `--ac-mono`.

## Standing rules

- **Scene layers are furniture, not content.** Every world's atmosphere lives in one `aria-hidden` layer stack (`.po-room`, `.sl-room`, …) painted below `z-index: 2` content; tested DOM never gains or loses nodes for scenery.
- **Meaning colors survive grayscale.** Hue is atmosphere on top of geometry/weight/position, never the only carrier of a verdict. Emerald still means "earned/true" wherever it appears.
- **One orchestrated entrance per world**, durations pinned by tests (1800–2500ms), always skippable, `useReducedMotion` short-circuits to `onDone()`.
- **Reduced motion**: every world CSS ends with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block; continuous loops stop rather than speed up.
- **The home is a hall of doorways.** Each `ActivityCard` carries a pure-CSS diorama of its world (`activity-scenes.css`, keyed by slug); the card's facts stay on the module paper below the scene. The intro borrows `--act-accent` from its world via the registry.
- **Registry accents** (`worlds/registry.tsx`) are each world's signature hue and feed the card eyebrow + intro accent.

## Unresolved

- Negation Station is not in `SEED_ACTIVITIES`, so its diorama only shows once assigned.
- Progress ornaments (chips, plaques, mileposts, seals…) remain unreachable from the runner; feeding them real verdicts is its own piece of work.

## Read, Recall, Colour door (locked 2026-09-05)

The two RRC doors (`SectionLibraryLaunch`, `SectionLaunch`, both drawn by `SectionLaunchFrame`) are a committed world of their own, mode **Persuade** at the top and **Operate** in the slab. Joshua's brief: a layered, high-end landing page in the spirit of heylemon.ai (cream ground, painted panels, serif display with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, a dictation demo) with fora.so's depth-plane hero, and bold step 1 to 4 instructions.

- **World:** one dusk. The runner's indigo (`#1a1635`) lifted into a sky over a painted meadow, cream paper (`#f6f3ea`) beneath. Display is Fraunces at opsz 144; body Inter; labels IBM Plex Mono. Marigold marks the Colour step, emerald marks a carried detail, nothing else is coloured.
- **Hero:** `MeadowScene` paints five depth planes generatively in SVG (seeded ridges, seeded grass blades, feTurbulence grain) so no raster ships; each plane rides `useScroll` at its own rate and the foreground grass crosses in front of the demo card. The headline blurs and recedes on the same scroll value.
- **Signature moment:** `RecallDemo`, a looping performance of the method on a synthetic stimulus, labelled as such, whose text literally dissolves at the Recall step.
- **Steps:** four cards, each with a small drawn scene, doubling as the ARIA tablist the tests already contract (`sl-beat`, `sl-beat-tab-N`, `sl-beat-panel`). The full briefing is the second tab of the drawer.
- **Tokens:** scoped to `.sl` as `--rd-*`. The design detector reports them as outside DESIGN.md; that is the sanctioned-world exception above, not drift.

## user

Tool result:
/**
 * Cutting one stimulus's speech into the LINES she was rephrasing.
 *
 * The drill is line by line: she reads one line silently, looks away, says that
 * line back in her own words, then returns to the text for the next one. The
 * app never asks her which line she is on (ADR 0013) and it never will -- a tap
 * per line is ninety taps a section and one mistimed tap desyncs the whole
 * recording. So the split is worked out AFTERWARDS, from two things the run
 * already leaves behind:
 *
 * - **The silence.** She is silent while reading, so every spoken line arrives
 *   as its own burst with a gap in front of it. Bursts are the candidate units,
 *   and nothing here assumes she talks continuously.
 * - **The order.** She works top to bottom, so bursts map to lines in order.
 *   That makes this an alignment, not a classification: a burst can only belong
 *   to the line it is nearest to in sequence, which is far more robust than
 *   asking which line a paraphrase "sounds like".
 *
 * Wording is the tiebreak, not the decision. A rephrase shares content words
 * with the line it came from, so overlap picks between the two lines an
 * ordering leaves ambiguous.
 *
 * Nothing in this file is a boundary she is GRADED against. Coverage stays per
 * stimulus (ADR 0013); this exists so she can be shown, and hear, which line
 * each part of her run belongs to.
 */

export interface TimedWord {
  word: string;
  /** Seconds from the start of the recording, as the transcriber reports them. */
  start: number;
  end: number;
}

export interface Utterance {
  text: string;
  startMs: number;
  endMs: number;
}

export interface LineSpan {
  /** 1-based line number within the stimulus, matching the authored chunk. */
  n: number;
  /** The line as written. */
  text: string;
  /** What she said for it, joined in order. Empty when she said nothing. */
  spoken: string;
  startMs?: number;
  endMs?: number;
}

/**
 * The gap that separates one spoken line from the next, in milliseconds.
 *
 * She has to look back at the screen and read the next line silently before she
 * speaks again, so the real gaps are seconds long. This is set below the
 * shortest of those and above the pauses inside a sentence; it is a default
 * rather than a constant because it is the one number here that wants tuning
 * against real recordings.
 */

}

/**
 * A preview harness for the section launch surface. Development only.
 *
 * It exists so the component can be looked at in a real browser, in every state
 * and at every breakpoint, without signing in and without a graded section
 * sitting in the database. Only the network is stubbed: `window.fetch` is
 * replaced before anything imports the Supabase client, so the component, the
 * client helper and the derivation all run exactly as they do in the product.
 *
 * Not routed and not imported by the app. Reached at
 * `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` on the dev server. It is not an entry
 * point in the Vite build, which only builds `index.html`, so it never ships.
 *
 * States: ?state=runs|oneRun|empty|failed|noHistory|library  ·  ?theme=light|dark
 */
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";

import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../features/activity-centre/api/types";

const state = params.get("state") ?? "runs";
const theme = params.get("theme") ?? "light";

function stim(item_order: number, over: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  return {
    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    item_order,
    carried: 3,
    total: 3,
    percent: 100,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0,
    spoken: "she said something here",
    ...over,
  };
}

/**
 * The Report Card, against the trial run's own numbers.
 *
 * The card is only reachable behind a signed-in student who has finished a
 * twenty-five minute recorded section and waited for the grader, which made it
 * the least-looked-at screen in the product and is how it shipped printing a
 * raw percentage as its hero directly under a docstring forbidding exactly
 * that. The fixture below is the PT102.S3 trial run: 91% mean Coverage, 17 of
 * 22 at the standard, one deliberate recite, one stimulus with seven seconds of
 * dead air, four uncoloured.
 *
 * The Clip button appears and does nothing here on purpose. There is no
 * recording behind a fixture, and a lab that faked one would be the one place
 * a broken player could hide.
 */
function ReportPanel() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState<"clean" | "faults" | "partial">("faults");

  const grade = useMemo(() => makeLabGrade(attempt), [attempt]);

  return (
    <div>
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 border-b border-border px-6 py-3">
        <Tabs
          options={["clean", "faults", "partial"] as const}
          value={attempt}
          onChange={(v) => setAttempt(v)}
          label="Run"
        />
        <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
          clean: everything at the standard · faults: the trial run · partial: stimuli we could not
          mark
        </p>
      </div>
      <div className="px-4 py-10">
        <SectionReportCard
          grade={grade}
          /*
           * The lab has no database, so the model runs are named rather than
           * looked up. The player button appears and does nothing, which is the
           * point: a lab that faked a working player would be the one place a
           * broken one could hide.
           *
           * The walkthrough is different, and is real text rather than a stub.
           * It is the part a student actually reads, it is the part most likely
           * to look wrong at some width or some length, and a lab that showed
           * the buttons but not the document would be checking the half that
           * matters least.
           */
          loadModelRuns={async () =>
            Object.fromEntries(
              grade.stimuli.map((st) => [
                st.slug,
                {
                  audio: { bucket: "model-runs", path: `${st.slug}.mp3` },
                  walkthrough:
                    "> Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so " +
                    "that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they " +
                    "do well.\n\n" +
                    "So a management consultant is telling us how to run the company, and " +
                    "the idea is to move people around so they are doing the things they " +

/*
 * The row that tells her what to change, and the standard beside her words.
 *
 * Every number on the card was a description. These are the three things a
 * student can act on: what to do next time, what the Read should have named,
 * and what the standard says for the very line she was rephrasing.
 */
describe("what to do next time", () => {
  it("shows the judge's advice on a stimulus with something to fix", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{ ...grade, stimuli: [stimulus({ nextTime: "Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some." })] }}
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(screen.getByText("Next time")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText("Say the quantifier out loud: all, not some.")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says every cylinder when there is nothing to fix, and gives no advice", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          stimuli: [
            stimulus({
              percent: 100,
              carried: 12,
              total: 12,
              missed: [],
              readGiven: true,
              readMatches: true,
              nextTime: "",
            }),
          ],
        }}
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(screen.getByText(/Every cylinder on this one/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText("Next time")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("does not call a recital every cylinder, whatever it carried", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          stimuli: [
            stimulus({ percent: 100, missed: [], readGiven: true, readMatches: true, reciting: true, nextTime: "" }),
          ],
        }}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\features\\activity-centre\\components\\report-card.css", "content": "/**\n * The Report Card's own world.\n *\n * Scoped entirely to `.rc`. Nothing here leaks, and the card borrows nothing\n * from the Activity Centre's cream surface or from the emerald that means\n * \"carried\" everywhere else in the wing. That is a decision, not drift\n * (Joshua, 2026-09-08): the cream-and-serif register reads as machine-written\n * now, and emerald had to carry meaning on a page where the student spends\n * twenty minutes reading her own words back.\n *\n * The scene it is built for: a student at nine in the evening, laptop open,\n * listening to her own voice against a recording of the same passage done\n * properly, working out what to fix before tomorrow. That forces two grounds,\n * not one.\n *\n * - **The console** is dark and lacquered. It holds the verdict, the transport\n *   and the ledger of twenty-two stimuli: the instrument, the parts she\n *   operates rather than reads.\n * - **The page** is cool paper. It holds everything she reads at length. Cool,\n *   not cream: a faint blue cast under the ink, so it reads as paper under a\n *   lamp rather than as a beige card.\n *\n * Verdicts are carried by fill, not by hue, so they survive grayscale and a\n * colour-blind reader: a solid mark held everything, a hollow ring lost it.\n * Exactly two hues are saturated, and each means one thing everywhere:\n *\n * - **Signal (indigo-violet)** is sound. It marks the line currently playing\n *   and nothing else, so the only saturated colour that moves on the page is\n *   the playhead.\n * - **Ember** is attention. It marks what slipped, and nothing else.\n */\n\n.rc {\n  /* ── The console ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n  --rc-console: oklch(0.21 0.018 268);\n  --rc-console-raised: oklch(0.26 0.02 268);\n  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.34 0.02 268);\n  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.96 0.005 268);\n  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.74 0.012 268);\n  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.58 0.014 268);\n\n  /* ── The page ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n  --rc-page: oklch(0.976 0.0035 268);\n  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.0015 268);\n  --rc-fill: oklch(0.955 0.005 268);\n  --rc-ink: oklch(0.24 0.02 268);\n  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.505 0.014 268);\n  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.645 0.012 268);\n  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.006 268);\n  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.94 0.005 268);\n\n  /* ── Meaning ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n  --rc-signal: oklch(0.55 0.19 286);\n  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.72 0.16 286);\n  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.945 0.032 286);\n  --rc-ember: oklch(0.54 0.15 42);\n  --rc-ember-soft: oklch(0.955 0.032 42);\n\n  --rc-display: \"Fraunces\", \"Iowan Old Style\", \"Times New Roman\", serif;\n  --rc-body: \"Lexend\", \"Outfit\", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;\n  --rc-mono: \"IBM Plex Mono\", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;\n\n  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about\n     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */\n  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n\n  background: var(--rc-page);\n  color: var(--rc-ink);\n  font-family: var(--rc-body);\n}\n\n/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped\n   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */\n.rc__measure {\n  margin-inline: auto;\n  max-width: 68ch;\n}\n\n/* ── Micro-labels ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   One label style for the whole card. Every section of a stimulus wears it,\n   which is what makes the order legible: she learns four labels once and then\n   knows where she is on all twenty-two. */\n.rc__label {\n  color: var(--rc-ink-faint);\n  font-family: var(--rc-mono);\n  font-size: 10px;\n  font-weight: 500;\n  letter-spacing: 0.14em;\n  text-transform: uppercase;\n}\n\n.rc__label--onconsole {\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);\n}\n\n.rc__display {\n  font-family: var(--rc-display);\n  font-variation-settings: \"opsz\" 96;\n  font-weight: 380;\n  letter-spacing: -0.02em;\n  line-height: 1.02;\n}\n\n/* ── The console ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n\n.rc__console {\n  background: var(--rc-console);\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink);\n}\n\n.rc__console-divider {\n  border-color: var(--rc-console-hairline);\n}\n\n/* ── The ledger of twenty-two ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n   The whole section as one row of marks, so the shape of the run is visible\n   before any one stimulus is opened. Twenty-two accordion rows are a list;\n   this is a picture. */\n.rc__ledger {\n  display: grid;\n  gap: 4px;\n  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(28px, 1fr));\n}\n\n.rc__tick {\n  align-items: center;\n  background: transparent;\n  border: 0;\n  border-radius: 6px;\n  cursor: pointer;\n  display: flex;\n  flex-direction: column;\n  gap: 5px;\n  /* 44px of target, reached by being 44px rather than by wrapped text. */\n  min-height: 44px;\n  justify-content: center;\n  padding: 4px 0;\n  transition: background-color 160ms var(--rc-ease);\n}\n\n.rc__tick:hover {\n  background: var(--rc-console-raised);\n}\n\n.rc__tick:focus-visible {\n  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal-bright);\n  outline-offset: 2px;\n}\n\n.rc__tick-n {\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);\n  font-family: var(--rc-mono);\n  font-size: 9px;\n  letter-spacing: 0.04em;\n  transition: color 160ms var(--rc-ease);\n}\n\n.rc__tick[aria-current=\"true\"] .rc__tick-n {\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink);\n}\n\n/* The mark itself. Fill is the verdict; there is no hue in any of the four. */\n.rc__mark {\n  border: 1.5px solid var(--rc-console-ink-dim);\n  border-radius: 3px;\n  display: block;\n  height: 18px;\n  position: relative;\n  width: 18px;\n}\n\n/* Held everything: solid. */\n.rc__mark--at-the-standard {\n  background: var(--rc-console-ink);\n  border-color: var(--rc-console-ink);\n}\n\n/* Most of it: filled from the floor, so the gap at the top is what she lost. */\n.rc__mark--nearly-there::after,\n.rc__mark--thin::after {\n  background: var(--rc-console-ink-dim);\n  bottom: 0;\n  content: \"\";\n  left: 0;\n  position: absolute;\n  right: 0;\n}\n\n.rc__mark--nearly-there::after {\n  height: 66%;\n}\n\n.rc__mark--thin::after {\n  height: 30%;\n}\n\n/* Lost it: a hollow ring in ember, the one place the ledger takes a hue. */\n.rc__mark--lost-it {\n  border-color: var(--rc-ember);\n  border-radius: 50%;\n}\n\n/* Said back word for word: an open mark with a bar through it. Not a worse\n   score, which is the whole point, so it takes no hue at all. */\n.rc__mark--recited {\n  border-radius: 50%;\n  border-style: dashed;\n}\n\n.rc__mark--ungraded {\n  border-style: dotted;\n  border-color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);\n}\n\n/* The stimulus currently open, marked on the ledger so she never loses her\n   place in twenty-two. */\n.rc__tick[aria-current=\"true\"] .rc__mark {\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--rc-console), 0 0 0 3.5px var(--rc-signal-bright);\n}\n\n/* ── The transport ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n\n.rc__transport {\n  align-items: center;\n  background: var(--rc-console-raised);\n  border-radius: 999px;\n  display: inline-flex;\n  gap: 2px;\n  padding: 3px;\n}\n\n.rc__source {\n  background: transparent;\n  border: 0;\n  border-radius: 999px;\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink-dim);\n  cursor: pointer;\n  font-family: var(--rc-body);\n  font-size: 12px;\n  font-weight: 500;\n  min-height: 34px;\n  padding: 0 14px;\n  transition:\n    background-color 180ms var(--rc-ease),\n    color 180ms var(--rc-ease);\n  white-space: nowrap;\n}\n\n.rc__source:hover {\n  color: var(--rc-console-ink);\n}\n\n.rc__source[aria-pressed=\"true\"] {\n  background: var(--rc-signal);\n  color: oklch(0.99 0.005 286);\n}\n\n.rc__source:focus-visible {\n  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal-bright);\n  outline-offset: 2px;\n}\n\n/* ── A line of her run ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n   The comparison the drill is about: the line as written, what she said for\n   it, and what the standard says for the same line. Stacked rather than in\n   columns, because the three are read in that order and a column layout makes\n   her scan sideways for a sentence she is meant to read straight through. */\n.rc__line {\n  border-top: 1px solid var(--rc-hairline-soft);\n  padding: 14px 0 14px 16px;\n  position: relative;\n  transition: background-color 220ms var(--rc-ease);\n}\n\n.rc__line:first-child {\n  border-top: 0;\n}\n\n/* The playhead. The only thing on the page that moves, and the only saturated\n   colour in the reading body while it does. */\n.rc__line::before {\n  background: var(--rc-signal);\n  border-radius: 2px;\n  bottom: 10px;\n  content: \"\";\n  left: 0;\n  opacity: 0;\n  position: absolute;\n  top: 10px;\n  transform: scaleY(0.4);\n  transform-origin: center;\n  transition:\n    opacity 200ms var(--rc-ease),\n    transform 320ms var(--rc-ease);\n  width: 3px;\n}\n\n.rc__line[data-playing=\"true\"] {\n  background: var(--rc-signal-soft);\n}\n\n.rc__line[data-playing=\"true\"]::before {\n  opacity: 1;\n  transform: scaleY(1);\n}\n\n/* The line as written. It is the anchor for the two beneath it, so it carries\n   the weight and they do not. */\n.rc__line-text {\n  color: var(--rc-ink);\n  font-size: 15px;\n  font-weight: 500;\n  line-height: 1.55;\n}\n\n.rc__line-spoken {\n  color: var(--rc-ink-muted);\n  font-size: 14.5px;\n  line-height: 1.6;\n}\n\n/* What she held and what she dropped, as a list of short phrases rather than\n   a semicolon-joined sentence. Nine phrases run together is a paragraph she\n   skims; nine rows is a thing she reads. */\n.rc__chips {\n  display: flex;\n  flex-wrap: wrap;\n  gap: 5px;\n}\n\n.rc__chip {\n  border-radius: 5px;\n  font-size: 12.5px;\n  line-height: 1.4;\n  padding: 3px 8px;\n}\n\n.rc__chip--held {\n  background: var(--rc-fill);\n  color: var(--rc-ink-muted);\n}\n\n.rc__chip--dropped {\n  background: var(--rc-ember-soft);\n  color: var(--rc-ember);\n}\n\n/* ── Playback affordance on a line ────────────────────────────────────── */\n\n.rc__hear {\n  align-items: center;\n  background: transparent;\n  border: 1px solid var(--rc-hairline);\n  border-radius: 999px;\n  color: var(--rc-ink-faint);\n  cursor: pointer;\n  display: inline-flex;\n  gap: 6px;\n  font-family: var(--rc-body);\n  font-size: 11px;\n  height: 26px;\n  padding: 0 10px;\n  transition:\n    border-color 160ms var(--rc-ease),\n    color 160ms var(--rc-ease);\n}\n\n.rc__hear:hover {\n  border-color: var(--rc-signal);\n  color: var(--rc-signal);\n}\n\n.rc__hear[data-playing=\"true\"] {\n  background: var(--rc-signal);\n  border-color: var(--rc-signal);\n  color: oklch(0.99 0.005 286);\n}\n\n.rc__hear:focus-visible {\n  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal);\n  outline-offset: 2px;\n}\n\n/* ── Motion ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .rc *,\n  .rc *::before,\n  .rc *::after {\n    animation: none !important;\n    transition-duration: 1ms !important;\n  }\n}\n"}

Four rules from the decisions behind it shape what is on screen:\n *\n * - **A band, not a percentage.** A correct run by the author of the standard\n *   sits at a median of 93% and clears 82% nine times in ten on the live\n *   judge (ADR 0035; ADR 0018 measured 95 and 86 by hand), so a raw score\n *   against an implied 100 tells a student she failed at something she did\n *   properly (ADR 0018). It is the statement that the number\n * beside it did not come from memory, so it takes no colour at all: the point is\n * the method, not a mark against her.\n */\nconst RECITED = { label: \"Said back word for word\", mark: \"recited\" };\n\nfunction minutes(seconds: number): string {\n  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);\n  const s = Math.round(seconds % 60);\n  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n}\n\n/**\n * Play one stimulus's stretch of the section recording, and say where it is.\n *\n * The whole run is one file and the trial proved a seek into it lands with zero\n * error, so a Clip is a start offset, an end offset and one audio element.\n * Nothing is cut server-side and nothing is downloaded twice.\n *\n * `positionMs` is the addition that makes the transcript follow the sound. The\n * grader already stores a start and an end for each line she spoke, so the card\n * does not need word timings to know which line is in the air: it needs the\n * playhead, and that is one number ticking off the element it already owns.\n *\n * The link is signed on first use rather than on mount: most Report Cards are\n * read without anyone pressing play, and a signed URL fetched for every card\n * that opens is a request per view for a file nobody asked for.\n */\nfunction useClip() {\n  const audio = useRef<HTMLAudioElement | null>(null);\n  const stopAt = useRef<number | null>(null);\n  const [playing, setPlaying] = useState<string | null>(null);\n  const [positionMs, setPositionMs] = useState<number>(0);\n  /** One signed link per file, made on first use and reused after. */\n  const urls = useRef<Map<string, Promise<string | null>>>(new Map());\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const el = audio.current;\n    return () => {\n      el?.pause();\n    };\n  }, []);\n\n  const stop = useCallback(() => {\n    audio.current?.pause();\n    stopAt.current = null;\n    setPlaying(null);\n  }, []);\n\n  const play = useCallback(\n    async (key: string, file: { bucket: string; path: string }, startMs = 0, endMs?: number) => {\n      if (playing === key) {\n        stop();\n        return;\n      }\n      const id = `${file.bucket}/${file.path}`;\n      if (!urls.current.has(id)) urls.current.set(id, signSectionRecording(file.bucket, file.path));\n      const src = await urls.current.get(id)!;\n      if (!src) return;\n      let el = audio.current;\n      if (!el) {\n        el = new Audio();\n        el.preload = \"none\";\n        // The end of a Clip is a time, not an event the element knows about, so\n        // it is watched for rather than waited on. The prompt asks it to leave\n * `next_time` empty when there is nothing to fix and it has never once done so\n * across 44 real stimuli, so a card that trusted the empty string would never\n * congratulate anybody.\n */\nfunction everyCylinder(s: SectionStimulusGrade): boolean {\n  return (\n    !s.ungraded &&\n    s.missed.length === 0 &&\n    s.readMatches === true &&\n    s.coloured &&\n    s.colourSpecific &&\n    !s.reciting &&\n    !s.listing\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * What she actually did well on this stimulus, in specifics.\n *\n * Praise is a specific observation or it is noise, and the card had none: a\n * stimulus at the standard got one sentence saying she carried every detail and\n * a stimulus at 86% got nothing at all, so twenty of twenty-two rows were a\n * list of what went wrong. (\n            <p key={i} className=\"text-sm italic leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]\">\n              {line.slice(2)}\n            </p>\n          ) : (\n            <p key={i} className=\"text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]\">\n              {line}\n            </p>\n          ),\n        )}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n/** One of the three figures on the console. */\nfunction Figure({ label, value, note }: { label: string; value: string; note?: string }) {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <p className=\"rc__label rc__label--onconsole\">{label}</p>\n      <p className=\"rc__display mt-1.5 text-[28px] text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]\">{value}</p>\n      {note && <p className=\"mt-1 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]\">{note}</p>}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n/** A section of the open stimulus. One label style, one rhythm, same order. */\nfunction Movement({\n  label,\n  children,\n  className,\n}: {\n  label: string;\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n  className?: string;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <section className={cn(\"border-t border-[var(--rc-hairline)] pt-5\", className)}>\n      <p className=\"rc__label\">{label}</p>\n      <div className=\"mt-3\">{children}</div>\n    </section>\n  );\n}\n\nexport function SectionReportCard({\n  grade,\n  onDone,\n  loadModelRuns = listModelRuns,\n}: SectionReportCardProps) {\n  const { section, stimuli } = grade;\n  /*\n   * One open at a time, and the first one open on arrival.\n   *\n   * The card used to open with twenty-two closed rows, which meant the default\n   * state of the screen she waited twenty-five minutes for was a list of\n   * headings. RECITED : BAND[s.band]) : null;\n  const clipWindow = s?.recall_window_ms ?? s?.window_ms;\n  const model = s ? modelRuns[s.slug] : undefined;\n  const pairs = model?.walkthrough ? walkthroughPairs(model.walkthrough) : [];\n  // Paired only when the standard's run has exactly one recall per authored\n  // line; anything else is shown whole, as before.\n  const pairedByLine = !!s?.lines?.length && pairs.length === s.lines.length;\n  const held = s ? whatHeld(s) : [];\n  /*\n   * The highlight follows her own voice only.\n   *\n   * The grader stores a start and an end for every line SHE spoke. This is the\n          * only one that asks for something, which is why it is one line and\n          * why a run with nothing clear to say gets none.\n          */}\n        {overall.next && (\n          <p className=\"mt-5 max-w-[58ch] border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)] pl-4 text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]\">\n            {overall.next}\n          </p>\n        )}\n\n        {/*\n          * The three figures.\n          *\n          * Coverage answers \"did you hold it\". The Read answers \"did you\n          * understand it\", and it is the criterion the method is built around.\n          * The clock is third because it is the one a student will read first\n          * if you let her, and it is the least important of the three.\n          */}\n        <div className=\"rc__console-divider mt-7 grid gap-6 border-t pt-6 sm:grid-cols-3\">\n          <Figure\n            label=\"Held\"\n            value={`${section.mean_coverage}%`}\n            note={\n              overall.sectionSize !== undefined\n                ? `of the details, across ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize}`\n                : \"of the details, said back\"\n            }\n          />\n          {overall.readMeasured && (\n            <Figure\n              label=\"Understood\"\n              value={`${section.read_matched_count} of ${section.stimuli_graded}`}\n              note={\n                (section.no_read_count ?? 0) > 0\n                  ? `Reads that found the move. You gave none at all on ${section.no_read_count}.`\n                  : \"Reads that found the move\"\n              }\n            />\n          )}\n          <div>\n            <p className=\"rc__label rc__label--onconsole\">Time</p>\n            <p className=\"rc__display mt-1.5 text-[28px] text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]\">\n              {minutes(section.total_seconds)}\n            </p>\n            <p className=\"mt-1 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]\">\n              against a {section.goal_minutes} minute goal\n            </p>\n            {/*\n              * The goal counts only if every stimulus was recalled, coloured and\n              * given a Read.   });

describe("the evidence behind the number", () => {
  const withSection = (over: Partial<SectionGrade["section"]>, stimuliOver?: SectionStimulusGrade[]) =>
    ({ ...grade, section: { ...grade.section, ...over }, stimuli: stimuliOver ?? grade.stimuli }) as SectionGrade;

  it("leads with the run's own band, not the percentage", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ mean_coverage: 91 })} />);
    // 91 is at the standard. The card used to print "91%" in 48px type with no
    // band at all, which is the exact thing its own docstring forbade.
    const hero = document.querySelector(".ac-display");
    expect(hero?.textContent).toBe("At the standard");
    expect(hero?.textContent).not.toMatch(/%/);
  });

  it("still says what she actually got, underneath", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ mean_coverage: 91 })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/91% of the details carried/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows The Read beside Coverage", () => {
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ read_given_count: 3, read_matched_count: 2, no_read_count: 0 })}
      />,
    );
    expect(screen.getByText("Understood")).toBeInTheDocument();
    // Against every stimulus graded, not against the ones she gave a Read on.
    expect(screen.getByText("2 of 4")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says the goal was missed on its condition, not on the clock", () => {
    // The spec's own rule: the goal counts only with every stimulus recalled,
    // coloured and given a Read. Finishing early with four skipped was being
    // reported as making the goal.
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ within_time: true, within_goal: false })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/Inside the time, but the goal only counts/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("says plainly when we could not mark part of the run", () => {
    render(<SectionReportCard loadModelRuns={noModelRuns} grade={withSection({ ungraded_count: 2 })} />);
    expect(screen.getByText(/2 stimuli could not be marked/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/ours, not yours/)).toBeInTheDocument();

  it("puts the standard's recall of a line under her own line, and shows what she held", async () => {
    const md =
      "> It is difficult to keep deep wounds free of bacteria.\n\nSo deep wounds are hard to keep clean.\n\n" +
      "> Even strong antibiotics fail.\n\nEven the strong drugs do not manage it.";
    render(
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={{
          ...grade,
          stimuli: [
            stimulus({
              spoken: "x",
              lines: [
                {
                  n: 1,
                  text: "It is difficult to keep deep wounds free of bacteria.",
                  spoken: "deep wounds are hard to keep clean",
                  verdict: "strong",
                  note: "",
                  carried: ["The wounds are deep."],
                  dropped: [],
                },
                {
                  n: 2,
                  text: "Even strong antibiotics fail.",
                  spoken: "antibiotics struggle",
                  verdict: "partial",
                  note: "Dropped that even the strong ones fail.",
                  carried: [],
                  dropped: ["Even the strong ones fail."],
                },
              ],
            }),
          ],
        }}
        loadModelRuns={() => Promise.resolve({ PT105_S2_Q1: { walkthrough: md, audio: null } })}
      />,
    );
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Stimulus 1"));
    expect(await screen.findByText(/So deep wounds are hard to keep clean/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/Even the strong drugs do not manage it/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText("The wounds are deep.")).toBeInTheDocument();
    // Paired under the lines, so the whole-run block is not repeated beneath.
    expect(screen.queryByText("Done right", { selector: "p" })).toBeNull();
  });
});

  // `over.slug` is the stimulus NUMBER, not the slug. Spread last it would
  // overwrite the slug it was meant to build, and the run history would then
  // look up Details for a stimulus called "7" and find none.
  const stim = ({ slug: n, ...over }: Partial<SectionStimulusGrade>): SectionStimulusGrade => ({

    carried: 20,
    total: 21,
    percent: 95,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,

    stimulus_text:
      "Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they do well. This, she says, will \"increase productivity by fully exploiting our available resources.\"",
    the_read: "What if exploit is doing two jobs in this argument?",
    readReason: "She said the consultant may be assuming everyone wants to move, which is a different move from the one the argument turns on.",
    nextTime:
      "Say the quantifier on purpose as you read: all employees, not the staff. It is the one word this argument leans on.",
    ...over,
  });

  const stimuli: SectionStimulusGrade[] =
    kind === "clean"
      ? Array.from({ length: 6 }, (_, i) => stim({ slug: String(i + 1) }))

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\firecrawl-deep-research

# Firecrawl Deep Research

Use this only for report-scale research: a rigorous, cited synthesis the user
explicitly wants delivered as a formal written report. If the request is a
product pick, a top-N list, a quick lookup, or anything answerable with a short
search, stop; do not use this skill, let the request be handled the standard
way.

This skill gathers its evidence from the open web. If the evidence base is the
published literature — a literature review, or a biomedical, clinical, or other
scientific topic where the answer lives in papers — use
[firecrawl-research-papers](../firecrawl-research-papers/SKILL.md) instead; it
queries Firecrawl's paper index rather than searching websites.

## Onboarding Interview

Infer the topic and output format from context. Before starting, unless already specified, always ask one short question to define the scope:

> "How long do you want this research task to run?"

Map the answer to a depth tier in the Collection Plan below:
- A few minutes → Quick
- ~10-15 minutes → Thorough
- Longer / no limit → Exhaustive

If the topic itself is unclear, you may ask at most 1-2 additional concise
questions (topic, or a critical angle/source constraint). Otherwise proceed once
the runtime is set.

## Firecrawl Collection Plan

Use Firecrawl search and scrape through the CLI or equivalent tool surface. Match
depth to the runtime the user chose during onboarding.

- Quick (~a few minutes): search 3-5 queries and scrape 5-10 high-quality sources.
- Thorough (~10-15 minutes): search 5-10 queries from different angles and scrape 15-25 sources.
- Exhaustive (longer): search 10+ queries and scrape 25+ sources, including primary sources, research papers, expert views, and contrarian sources.

Avoid re-scraping URLs already returned with full content from a search-with-scrape result.

### When Published Papers Are The Evidence

Search and scrape reach web pages. They do not query Firecrawl's research paper
index, which holds paper abstracts with full text reachable per paper — largely
biomedical and life-science literature from PubMed, bioRxiv, and medRxiv, plus
arXiv preprints in CS, physics, and math.

Hand off to [firecrawl-research-papers](../firecrawl-research-papers/SKILL.md)
when the report's evidence base is the published literature — a biomedical,
clinical, drug, gene, disease, epidemiology, or public-health topic, or any
request phrased as a literature review, systematic review, or survey of studies.
That skill uses `firecrawl_research_*` (MCP) / `firecrawl research` (CLI) to
search abstracts, expand to related papers, and verify claims inside a paper
body — none of which plain search and scrape can do.

If the report needs both — the literature *and* market, policy, or news context —
run the paper work through that skill and keep the web collection above for the
rest, then synthesize here.

Note that passing `categories: ["research"]` to Firecrawl search does not query
the paper index either. It filters an ordinary web search to research-affiliated
websites — the list includes PubMed, bioRxiv, medRxiv, arXiv, and publisher
sites — and returns their web pages, not the paper records behind them.

## Parallel Work

If appropriate, use sub-agents or equivalent parallel task runners by research angle:

- overview and definitions
- technical or implementation details
- market and industry context
- contrarian views, risks, and limitations
- primary sources and official docs

Each researcher should return claims, source URLs, source quality notes, and uncertainty.

## Final Deliverable

Default structure:

```markdown
# Deep Research: [Topic]

## Executive Summary
[2-3 paragraphs]

## Key Findings
[Numbered findings with source links]

## Detailed Analysis
[Themes, evidence, and synthesis]

## Contrarian Views And Risks
[Counterarguments, limitations, failure modes]

## Open Questions
[What remains uncertain]

## Sources
[Every URL used with a one-line note]

## Rerun Inputs
workflow: firecrawl-deep-research
topic: [topic]
depth: [quick/thorough/exhaustive]
output: [markdown/json/brief]
```

## Quality Bar

- Cite sources for factual claims.
- Prefer primary sources when available.
- Flag uncertainty and conflicting evidence.
- Synthesize instead of listing scrape summaries.

Record component names and what makes each visually distinctive.\n2. **Transcript-synced playback UI in the wild.** Look at how Descript, Otter, Sonix, Trint, Happy Scribe, and Gong present a transcript that highlights as audio plays: what carries the highlight (background fill, underline, a moving bar, word-level vs line-level), what colour it is, and how the \"currently playing\" state is distinguished from \"selected\".\n3. **Scorecard / rubric / feedback UI.** How do Gong, Chorus, Grammarly, Duolingo and any language-learning app present a per-item verdict with colour? Specifically: what colours mean \"good\" when green is unavailable, and how do they avoid a page of misses reading as a wall of red?\n\nReport back with:\n- A list of concrete, named component patterns worth stealing, each with: what it is, where you saw it, and the specific visual mechanic (not \"it looks clean\").\n- The transcript-highlight mechanics table: product, granularity, highlight treatment, colour.\n- Recommendations for the 3 hardest bits: the 22-item ledger, the playing-line highlight, and per-item verdict colour.\n- Every source URL.\n\nBe concrete and visual. Do not write code."}

CLI tool for Firecrawl web scraping

Options:
  -V, --version                                output the version number

  --api-url <url>                              API URL (or set FIRECRAWL_API_URL env var)
  --status                                     Show version, auth status, concurrency, and credits
  -h, --help                                   display help for command

Commands:
  scrape [options] [urls...]                   Scrape one or more URLs. Multiple URLs are scraped concurrently and saved to .firecrawl/
  crawl [options] [url-or-job-id]              Crawl a website using Firecrawl
  map [options] [url]                          Map URLs on a website using Firecrawl
  parse [options] <file>                       Parse a local file (HTML, PDF, DOCX, DOC, ODT, RTF, XLSX, XLS) into markdown, HTML, links, JSON, and more. Uses /v2/parse.
  monitor                                      Schedule recurring scrapes/crawls/searches and track content changes
  search [options] <query>                     Search the web using Firecrawl
  developer [options] <query>                  Search an index built for coding agents: GitHub issues, merged PRs, repository READMEs, and curated documentation sites. Express repository, source, language, topic, license, and other scoping intent in the query text; semantic retrieval handles the scoping.
  research                                     Search Firecrawl's research paper index: ~43M abstracts, around 90% biomedical (PubMed, bioRxiv, medRxiv) plus arXiv. Use this for biomedical, clinical, and scientific literature instead of scraping PubMed, bioRxiv, or Google Scholar by hand. Also searches GitHub issue/PR history.
  feedback [options] <endpoint> <jobId>        Send feedback on a Firecrawl endpoint job.
  search-feedback [options] <searchId>         Send feedback on a previous search result. Refunds 1 credit on first submission.
  agent [options] <prompt-or-job-id>           Run an AI agent to extract data from the web
  interact [options] [args...]                 Interact with a scraped page in a live browser session. Run AI prompts or execute code against any previous scrape.
  experimental|x                               Experimental commands (download)
  config [options]                             Configure Firecrawl (login if not authenticated)
  view-config                                  View current configuration and authentication status
  login [options]                              Login to Firecrawl (alias for config)
  logout                                       Logout and clear stored credentials
  init [options] [template]                    Set up Firecrawl: install CLI, authenticate, add integrations, and scaffold a template
  setup [options] [subcommand]                 Set up individual firecrawl integrations (core, build, workflows, mcp, defaults)
  make [options] <target>                      Make Firecrawl the default provider for supported workflows
  launch|launcher [options] [agent] [args...]  Configure Firecrawl MCP for an AI agent, then launch it
  env [options]                                Pull FIRECRAWL_API_KEY into a local .env file
  credit-usage [options]                       Get team credit usage information
  doctor [options] [job-id]                    Run environment diagnostics, or diagnose a specific run by job ID
  version [options]                            Display version information
  help [command]                               display help for command

A natural phrase for how much came back, replacing the Detail arithmetic.\nanchor = '''/**\n * What she actually did well on this stimulus, in specifics.'''\nhelper = '''/**\n * How much of it came back, in words.\n *\n * A Detail is OUR unit. It is how the judge is asked to mark, it is what the\n * coverage percentage is computed from, and a student has never seen one\n * enumerated: she read a passage and said it back. Printing \"20 of 21 details\"\n * hands her our machinery and asks her to care about the one number on the page\n * she cannot check (Joshua, 2026-09-08). The band says how it went; this says\n * how much of it she kept, the way a person would say it.\n */\nfunction howMuch(percent: number): string {\n  if (percent >= 100) return \"All of it came back.\";\n  if (percent >= 90) return \"Almost all of it came back.\";\n  if (percent >= 75) return \"Most of it came back.\";\n  if (percent >= 55) return \"About half of it came back.\";\n  if (percent >= 25) return \"Some of it came back.\";\n  return \"Not much of it came back.\";\n}\n\n/**\n * What she actually did well on this stimulus, in specifics.'''\nassert anchor in t; t=t.replace(anchor, helper, 1)\n\n# 2. whatHeld drops the count line entirely and keeps the qualitative specifics.\na = '''  if (!s.reciting) {\n    // Nothing about coverage here when she dropped nothing: \"What slipped\"\n    // says exactly that, directly below, and the two sections sat next to each\n    // other stating the same fact in two different sentences.\n    if (s.missed.length > 0 && s.total > 0 && s.carried / s.total >= 0.8)\n      held.push(`${s.carried} of the ${s.total} details came back.`);\n    const whole = s.lines?.filter((l) => l.verdict === \"strong\").length ?? 0;\n    if (whole > 0) held.push(`${whole} ${whole === 1 ? \"line\" : \"lines\"} came back whole.`);\n  }'''\nb = '''  if (!s.reciting) {\n    // How much came back is said once, on the row above, in words. It is not\n    // repeated here as a fraction.\n    const whole = s.lines?.filter((l) => l.verdict === \"strong\").length ?? 0;\n    if (whole === 1) held.push(\"One line came back whole.\");\n    else if (whole > 1 && whole === s.lines?.length) held.push(\"Every line came back whole.\");\n    else if (whole > 1) held.push(`${whole} of the lines came back whole.`);\n  }'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\n\n# 3. The stimulus header says how much came back rather than counting Details.\na = '''              <p className=\"text-sm tabular-nums text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]\">\n                {s.carried} of {s.total} details\n              </p>'''\nb = '''              <p className=\"text-sm text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]\">{howMuch(s.percent)}</p>'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\n\n# 4. The console figures lose the word \"details\".\na = '''                : \"of the details, said back\"'''\nb = '''                : \"of what was on the page, said back in your own words\"'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\na = '''                ? `of the details, across the ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} that saved`'''\nb = '''                ? `said back in your own words, across the ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} that saved`'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\na = '''                  ? `Reads that found the move. You gave none at all on ${section.no_read_count}.`\n                  : \"Reads that found the move\"'''\nb = '''                  ? `where you found the move. On ${section.no_read_count} you did not give a Read at all.`\n                  : \"where you found the move the argument turns on\"'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\n\n# 5. \"What slipped\" with nothing missed no longer needs to count either.\na = '''                        {s.reciting\n                          ? \"Every detail is in there, which is what reciting does rather than what you did.\"\n                          : \"You carried every detail in this one.\"}'''\nb = '''                        {s.reciting\n                          ? \"It is all in there, which is what saying it back word for word does rather than what you did.\"\n                          : \"Nothing slipped on this one. You kept the whole thing.\"}'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\n\na = '''                        Every cylinder on this one: every detail, the Read, and a reaction of your\n                        own, in your own words.'''\nb = '''                        Every cylinder on this one. You kept the whole thing, you found the move it\n                        turns on, and you had something of your own to say about it, all in your own\n                        words.'''\nassert a in t; t=t.replace(a,b)\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(t)\nprint(\"component ok\")\nPY", "description": "Replace Detail arithmetic with natural language"}

## assistant

Six Firecrawl agent jobs are running server-side. While they finish, here's where things stand.

**Done and verified**

The Report Card is rebuilt. 52/52 of its own tests pass, 2,685 across the Activity Centre, typecheck clean, and every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser.

- **Structure**: 22 collapsible rows became a ledger of 22 marks on a console, plus one stimulus open at a time in a fixed order — the passage, what you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time. Same order every time, so by the third she stops reading the labels.
- **Playback**: the transcript follows the sound. The grader already stored a start and end for each line she spoke, so no word timings and no re-grade were needed — the playhead drives a highlight down the lines as her recording plays. Her voice and the standard's sit in one transport rather than two scattered links.
- **Specific congratulation**: a "What you held" section read off measured facts, ahead of the correction rather than after it.

**Just changed on your note about details**

"20 of 21 details" is gone everywhere. A Detail is our unit — she never saw one enumerated — so it now reads "Almost all of it came back", "Nothing slipped on this one. You kept the whole thing." The console says "of what was on the page, said back in your own words" instead of "of the details".

**One correction to something I told you earlier**

I said the "Every cylinder" branch was dead code. That was wrong. It gates on measured criteria, not on the judge's empty string, so it fires correctly — Celia never triggers it because she gave no Read on any of the 22, which is the right answer. The real finding is narrower: the judge never returns an empty `next_time`, but the card doesn't depend on that.

**Still open**

The colour pass. You're right that it's too grey — I built it near-monochrome on the reasoning that verdicts should survive grayscale, and that's too austere for what you asked for. The most useful thing to come back so far is Grammarly's mechanic: colour by *category* of feedback, not by severity. Blue for clarity, green for engagement, purple for tone, red only for outright errors. That's how a page of feedback stays colourful and informative instead of becoming a heat map of failure — and it maps directly onto what this card already distinguishes: a dropped Detail, a missed Read, a missing reaction, a recital.

I'll wait for the palette and accessibility jobs before committing to hexes rather than guess at them.

## assistant

The accessibility research gives me what I needed most: Material 3's baseline is a violet-tinted neutral system engineered for AA — `#fffbfe` paper, `#6750a4` primary, `#7d5260` plum tertiary. That's not cream, not grey, and violet-as-a-field is on the underused list rather than the reflex list.

The **page** is a\n * violet-cast white holding everything she reads at length.\n *\n * ── Why plum and violet ──────────────────────────────────────────────────\n *\n * Researched rather than picked (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). ## What the research changed

The Firecrawl jobs indicted two of my own choices. The hue families currently saturated in award-winning work are acid yellow, electric cobalt, **orange and coral**, warm sand and terracotta, and **black plus one neon accent** — which is exactly what I'd built. Under-used: violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. With green and cream vetoed by you, violet and plum are what's left that's both fresh and serious.

The single most useful finding was Grammarly's mechanic: it colours by **category** (blue for clarity, green for engagement, purple for tone) and reserves red for outright errors. That's what keeps a page of feedback from becoming a heat map of failure — and it maps onto what this card already distinguishes.

So the four tones are now fixed and each means a *kind* of feedback, never a severity:

- **Detail** plum-rose — what she was holding, and what slipped out of it
- **Read** teal — whether she found the move the argument turns on
- **Colour** gold — what she brought of her own, and what she held
- **Method** indigo — reciting, listing, going quiet

A miss is the colour of the thing it's a miss *of*. Magenta is the one exception and means sound: the line playing, nothing else. Band stays carried by fill rather than hue, so the ledger still survives grayscale.

Neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary) — a system built to hold AA rather than a mood board.

## Detail counts are gone

"20 of 21 details" is out everywhere. A Detail is our grading unit; she's never seen one enumerated. It reads "Almost all of it came back" now, and the console says "of what was on the page, said back in your own words".

## Verified

96 test files, 2,685 tests, typecheck clean. Every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser across the default, playing and clean states — 34 samples, zero failures. Screenshots at 1180px and 390px.

## The push is blocked, and it isn't mine to clear

`origin/main` already has `c8e4478 Fixed broken style rule`, but the working tree has an uncommitted edit to `src/components/questionfinder/questionFinder.css` from your other session — caught mid-keystroke, line 119 is `.qf-trigger-label,` with nothing after it. That's what was 500ing the dev server earlier. safe-push refuses to merge over it, and I won't stash or discard someone else's in-flight work.

Tell me to drop that local change and I'll push immediately; upstream appears to have already fixed the same file.

Two Firecrawl jobs are still running (education results screens, and the best non-green colour for "correct"). They may sharpen the gold, but nothing is waiting on them.

## user

Tool result:
oose the blue family for a correct-answer state, using a darker accessible blue in production rather than pure #0000FF. Blue is the best compromise when green is unavailable: it is familiar, calm, color-vision-friendly, and visually distinct from the red/orange error family, while being less warning-like than amber/coral and less culturally or semantically loaded than violet, plum, gold, or pink. The main ambiguity—blue meaning link, focus, selection, or information—must be removed with a checkmark, explicit ‘Correct’ text, a distinct background/border treatment, and non-color cues. If the product already uses blue for primary actions or selection, use teal as the fallback, but test it because teal can still collapse into green.

## What the last two research jobs said

**On colour:** the recommendation was blue for a "correct" state, teal as fallback, with an explicit warning that gold "can mean premium, money, rarity, or rank rather than simply correct." That reads like it indicts the gold, but it doesn't apply here — that advice is for a binary correct/incorrect token, and this card deliberately has no such token. Gold isn't "correct" on the Report Card, it's the *Colour* category: what she brought of her own. In the ledger, where gold fill does encode band, "achievement or rank" is the right reading. The one thing worth checking was the warning that teal can collapse into green given your emerald veto, and it doesn't: my Read teal sits at hue 218, which is blue-cyan. Green is around 150. **No rework.**

**On education results screens:** one real defect, now fixed. Every product that does spoken feedback well surfaces a small actionable set rather than every imperfection, and Busuu names three. The grader has been returning exactly that in `bigMisses` since it was written, and the card rendered it as a single line of grey text at the foot of the line-by-line block, under every chip and panel. It now leads "What slipped", with the full list of missed Details folded underneath.

## What I'd do next, in order

1. **Look at it as Celia.** Everything I've verified is against a fixture. The card has never been seen with a real recording behind it, so the one thing genuinely unproven is the playhead: whether the stored line timings actually land where her voice is. That needs a student login, which I don't have.
2. **The retry path.** The education research was consistent that a miss should come with a way to try again. The console ends on a sentence about what to do tomorrow and a Done button. There's a redrill test in the runner, so something may exist to wire up.
3. **The `next_time` voice pass.** Still outstanding from this morning: 11 em-dashes across 44 lines, "Push past just retelling" ten times. That's the judge's wording, not the card's, and it needs a prompt change plus a deploy.

Loose end: `.claude/launch.json` still has my unused `rrc-report` entry uncommitted. Say the word and I'll revert it.

Under-used: violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty rose as a field, cool neutrals. Orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used list, so it moved. Neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is (`#fffbfe`, `#7d5260`), a system built to hold AA. A later job recommended blue for a generic \"correct\" token and warned gold reads as rank; it does not apply, because this card has no correct/incorrect token and gold is a category, not a verdict.\n\n**`bigMisses` promoted.** The grader has returned at most three plain-words misses since it was written and the card printed them as one grey line at the foot of the line-by-line block. They now lead \"What slipped\", with the full missed list folded under. Every spoken-feedback product that does this well surfaces a small actionable set (Busuu names three).\n\n**Verified:** 96 test files / 2,685 Activity Centre tests, typecheck clean, and every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser across the default, playing and clean states (34 samples, 0 failures) via `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs`, which converts computed colours through a canvas because Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`. It's ready to publish. Everything is on `origin/main`, and I verified the production build rather than inferring it from the typecheck.

**Build proven, not assumed**

`vite build` succeeds in 38s. Two things I checked specifically:

- `report-card-DSjXK5rK.css` is emitted as its own chunk with `--rc-console: oklch(.245 .058 312)` intact, so the plum world actually ships.
- The preview harness does **not** ship. No `dist/src`, and nothing in `dist/` references `reportCardPreview`. Vite only builds `index.html`, so the dev-only entry stays out of production exactly as intended.

Lint clean on both changed files. 2,685 tests and typecheck already green.

One thing worth knowing: `npm run build` fails on this Windows box because the script uses a Unix `NODE_OPTIONS=` prefix that npm hands to cmd.exe. That's pre-existing and has nothing to do with this change — Lovable builds on Linux, where it's fine. I built with the variable set properly to get a real result.

**Nothing else to deploy**

The diff touches no `supabase/` files, so there's no edge function to push through Lovable's agent — which matters, because functions don't deploy from a git push. And no student copy moved into Postgres; every string I changed lives in the component, so no `content:push` either. This is a pure frontend publish.

**The one thing to check before you hit Publish**

Lovable is the source of truth and its working copy has drifted from GitHub before, in this very project. Confirm its HEAD is at or past `a9cb6e2` before publishing. `origin/main` is currently `1335713` (another session's Testing Centre work sits on top of mine), and both my commits — `3bd7aba` and `a9cb6e2` — are ancestors of it.

**What you'll be shipping unverified**

The playhead has still never run against a real recording. Everything I verified is against the fixture, because I have no student login. The line timings come from data already in Celia's grade row, so the mechanism is sound, but the first real proof will be the first student who opens a card and presses play.

      if (cancelled) return;

## assistant

Pushed as `7939bd0`. Asking that question caught two real defects that my harness was hiding.

**What she actually gets after finishing the drill**

I traced the path: `ActivityRunner` phase `report` → `SectionReport` → `SectionReportCard`. Both render sites import the same component, so there's no stale second copy of the card anywhere — the admin attempt view (`ActivityResults`) gets the rebuild too.

**Two things were broken on that path, and only on that path**

1. **The card was floating on warm cream.** The report phase is wrapped in `.activity-centre`, and that class paints `hsl(38 36% 93%)`. So the plum card a student sees at the end of a drill was sitting on exactly the surface you told me to get away from. I'd verified everything on a neutral harness field no student ever sees. The phase now brings its own ground, one step darker than the card's page so the card still reads as a card.

2. **Every focusable thing in the card wore an emerald ring.** `.activity-centre :focus-visible` forces emerald on everything inside the module — the ledger ticks, the transport, every "Hear it" button — on the one card whose brief was no emerald. Measured it before and after: the ring is now plum and magenta, `ringHasEmerald: false`.

Both overrides are compounded onto `.activity-centre` rather than left bare, because a lone `.rc-ground` ties it on specificity and the winner would then depend on stylesheet order across a code-split build.

**Verified**

96 files / 2,685 tests, typecheck clean, production build succeeds in 44s, and I confirmed `rc-ground{background:#e9e4ed}` and the console focus rule are both present in the shipped `report-card-B3QMfZle.css`. Contrast re-measured inside the real wrapper: 29 samples, zero failures.

The harness now has `?frame=runner`, which wraps the card in the exact element the runner uses. That's the mode I should have been checking against from the start.

**Still true**

The playhead has never run against a real recording — no student login. Everything else about the completion path is now verified against the real frame rather than a convenient one.

   *
   * Its own Report Card is what opens: a completed section routes there rather

  onOpenAttempt?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /** Injected in tests. */
  loadChoices?: () => Promise<SectionChoice[]>;

  beginAttempt?: (activityId: string) => Promise<string>;
}

function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
}

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.
 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.
 */
function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}

}: SectionLibraryProps) {

        const kind = FEED_KIND[row.kind];

          .filter(Boolean)
          .join(" · ");

          key: row.key,

## assistant

Fixed and pushed (`01f3621`). Celia's report was accurate, and the diagnosis is a bit humbling.

**Nothing was actually broken.** The routing has been correct for weeks: her attempt `3afbbbde` is `completed` / `section` / `spoken-recall`, PT1.S3 is a listed section, and `/activities/:id` reopens a completed section straight into its Report Card. The button she needed was already there and already pointed at the right place.

**It just didn't look like a button.** The attempt row was 13.5px muted text with a dotted underline, no border, no padding — visually a caption. And the label stopped at the number: `Attempt 1 · 8 Sep · 87% carried`. She read the score off it and reasonably concluded that was all there was. Her sentence is almost a description of the CSS.

So the row is a control now: bordered, full width, 44px target, and it names where it goes.

> Attempt 2 · 2 Sept · 91% carried — **See what you missed**

A run still being marked says "Check back shortly" rather than going silent. An unfinished one stays flat and dashed with no action.

2,686 tests pass, typecheck clean. It got a regression test, because a student found this and the suite didn't.

**One gap I did not close, and you should know about it**

`useClassroomHome.ts:184` filters `row.status !== "completed"`, so the moment she finishes a section its row disappears from Classroom. That's right for a to-do list, but an RRC Report Card is produced *minutes after* she stops — completed is exactly when it becomes worth opening. Her only route is now Classroom → the section-library row → PT1.S3 → the attempt row. Three levels, with nothing telling her a card exists.

The complete fix is a "your Report Card is ready" row in Classroom when a grade lands. That's a more invasive change to the feed, so I didn't do it unasked — but it's the difference between her being able to find it and her being told it's there.

**Worth replying to her now:** her card is live at PT1.S3 in the section library, and she's at 87% with 17 of 22 at the standard. Want me to draft that?

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\components\\classroom\\home\\reportCardSeen.ts", "content": "/**\n * Whether she has already opened a Report Card.\n *\n * A graded section needs to reach her home page, because grading finishes\n * minutes after she stops and nothing else on the site tells her the card\n * exists. Celia sat a section, saw a percentage, and asked where the graded\n * responses were (2026-09-08). But a prompt that stays after it has been acted\n * on is worse than no prompt: \"Assigned to you\" is a list of things still to\n * do, and a card she has read is not one of them.\n *\n * Kept in `localStorage` rather than on the row. `activity_assignments` has no\n * column for it, and production schema changes here go through their own path\n * rather than through a migration file in this repo, so a column is not\n * something this change can add and prove. The cost is honest and small: the\n * flag is per browser, so on a second device she is nudged once more and then\n * it settles. Nothing about her work depends on it, and the permanent record\n * is the Section Library, which lists every Attempt for as long as it exists.\n *\n * Two independent reasons a card leaves the home page, so a cleared browser\n * cannot make one linger for ever:\n *\n * - she opened it, or\n * - it went stale. A Report Card is worth interrupting her for on the night it\n *   lands and the morning after, not a fortnight later.\n */\n\nconst KEY = \"lsatu:rrc:report-card-seen\";\n\n/** A card she should still be told about is fresh AND unopened. */\nexport const REPORT_CARD_FRESH_MS = 3 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;\n\nfunction read(): Record<string, number> {\n  try {\n    const raw = localStorage.getItem(KEY);\n    if (!raw) return {};\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);\n    return parsed && typeof parsed === \"object\" ? (parsed as Record<string, number>) : {};\n  } catch {\n    // Private windows, cleared site data, and browsers set to block storage all\n    // land here. Behaving as though nothing was seen shows her one extra nudge,\n    // which is the right way round to be wrong.\n    return {};\n  }\n}\n\n/** One card, identified the way she experiences it: this attempt at this run. */\nexport function reportCardKey(assignmentId: string, attemptNo: number): string {\n  return `${assignmentId}:${attemptNo}`;\n}\n\nexport function hasSeenReportCard(assignmentId: string, attemptNo: number): boolean {\n  return reportCardKey(assignmentId, attemptNo) in read();\n}\n\nexport function markReportCardSeen(assignmentId: string, attemptNo: number): void {\n  try {\n    const seen = read();\n    seen[reportCardKey(assignmentId, attemptNo)] = Date.now();\n    localStorage.setItem(KEY, JSON.stringify(seen));\n  } catch {\n    // Not being able to remember is survivable: she sees the row once more.\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Worth putting on her home page.\n *\n * Exported so the rule lives in one place rather than being re-derived at the\n * call site, and so a test can state it without a browser.\n */\nexport function shouldPromptReportCard(\n  gradedAt: string,\n  assignmentId: string,\n  attemptNo: number,\n  now: number = Date.now(),\n): boolean {\n  if (hasSeenReportCard(assignmentId, attemptNo)) return false;\n  const landed = new Date(gradedAt).getTime();\n  if (!Number.isFinite(landed)) return false;\n  return now - landed < REPORT_CARD_FRESH_MS;\n}\n"}

   * matter. The order is the page's argument: the top row is what to do next.
   */
  const rows = React.useMemo(() => {
    const ranked = [...workRows].sort((a, b) => {
      const rank = (row: HomeRow) => (row.overdue ? 0 : row.dueToday ? 1 : 2);
      const delta = rank(a) - rank(b);
      if (delta !== 0) return delta;
      return a.isNew === b.isNew ? 0 : a.isNew ? -1 : 1;
    });
    // Unread reading matter goes above ordinary work but below anything
    // overdue or due today. Appending it after every assignment looked right
    // and was not: Home shows four rows, so a student with four open
    // assignments never saw a new note at all, which is the case this whole
    // feature exists for.
    const urgent = ranked.filter((row) => row.overdue || row.dueToday);
    const rest = ranked.filter((row) => !(row.overdue || row.dueToday));
    const unreadReading = readingRows.filter((row) => row.isNew);
    const readReading = readingRows.filter((row) => !row.isNew);
    const all = [...urgent, ...unreadReading, ...rest, ...readReading];
    if (all.length) all[0] = { ...all[0], emphasis: true };
    return all;
  }, [readingRows, workRows]);

  // The hero takes the top row. The list below repeats it deliberately: the
  // hero is the argument for doing it now, the list is the inventory, and a
  // student scanning only the list should still find the thing that is due.
  const heroRow = rows[0] ?? null;

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\src\\components\\classroom\\home\\reportCardSeen.test.ts", "content": "import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport {\n  REPORT_CARD_FRESH_MS,\n  hasSeenReportCard,\n  markReportCardSeen,\n  shouldPromptReportCard,\n} from \"./reportCardSeen\";\n\n/**\n * A graded section has to reach her home page, and then stop.\n *\n * Celia sat a section, saw a percentage, and asked where the graded responses\n * were: grading finishes minutes after she stops, by which point the run has\n * left \"Assigned to you\" because it is completed. So the card announces itself\n * there. The other half matters just as much: \"Assigned to you\" is work still\n * outstanding, and a card she has read is not outstanding.\n */\ndescribe(\"when a Report Card is worth her home page\", () => {\n  const iso = (ms: number) => new Date(ms).toISOString();\n  const NOW = Date.parse(\"2026-09-08T09:00:00Z\");\n\n  beforeEach(() => localStorage.clear());\n\n  it(\"prompts for a card she has not opened\", () => {\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(iso(NOW - 60_000), \"a1\", 1, NOW)).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it(\"stops the moment she opens it, so it does not linger as work to do\", () => {\n    markReportCardSeen(\"a1\", 1);\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(iso(NOW - 60_000), \"a1\", 1, NOW)).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it(\"keeps each Attempt separate, because a second run earns a second card\", () => {\n    markReportCardSeen(\"a1\", 1);\n    expect(hasSeenReportCard(\"a1\", 1)).toBe(true);\n    expect(hasSeenReportCard(\"a1\", 2)).toBe(false);\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(iso(NOW - 60_000), \"a1\", 2, NOW)).toBe(true);\n  });\n\n  it(\"goes quiet on its own once the card is old, even if she never opened it\", () => {\n    // The flag lives in this browser, so a cleared one would otherwise nag for\n    // ever. A Report Card is news on the night it lands, not a fortnight later.\n    const stale = NOW - REPORT_CARD_FRESH_MS - 1;\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(iso(stale), \"a1\", 1, NOW)).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it(\"says no rather than throwing when the date is unusable\", () => {\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(\"not a date\", \"a1\", 1, NOW)).toBe(false);\n  });\n\n  it(\"treats unreadable storage as nothing seen, which errs toward showing her\", () => {\n    localStorage.setItem(\"lsatu:rrc:report-card-seen\", \"{ not json\");\n    expect(hasSeenReportCard(\"a1\", 1)).toBe(false);\n    expect(shouldPromptReportCard(iso(NOW - 60_000), \"a1\", 1, NOW)).toBe(true);\n  });\n});\n"}
