## What's happening

Ivory's account has **`has_activity_access: false`**. He's the only one of the nine students with RRC assigned who is missing that flag:

| Student | activity | assignments |
|---|---|---|
| Steven, Fernande, Sofia, Celia, Yahunathan, Charles, Ore | true | 4–9 each |
| **Ivory Johnson** | **false** | 3 |

He does have `has_classroom_access: true`, which is why he can sign in and reach the classroom — and why the failure looks like a broken link rather than a locked account.

That one flag explains **both** of his emails:

1. **Yesterday** ("I don't see the assigned work on my portal"). Classroom home filters activity rows out for anyone without the flag — `useClassroomHome.ts:189`. His three RRC assignments were invisible to him.
2. **Today** (the screenshot). `/activities/:id` sits behind `ProtectedRoute flag="has_activity_access"`, so it renders "Not open yet — This part of the classroom isn't in your plan right now." That's our own gate doing exactly what it was told.

He has three RRC sections assigned, all created today, all `status=assigned`: PT1.S3, PT21.S3, PT101.S2.

## The fix

Flip `has_activity_access` to `true` on his profile (`class_id 59790b57-7bd9-46a2-bab2-35c606f4ecb2`). One field. It's toggleable from the Admin dashboard's per-feature controls, or I can run the update.

I haven't changed it — that's a permission on a real student's account, so I'd rather you say go. Want me to?

Worth noting the copy did its job here: the error told him to message you, and he did. But he'd already lost a day thinking the portal was empty.

**On "specifically for that drill"** — the permission system doesn't have per-drill granularity. `has_activity_access` is one boolean for the whole Activity Centre; there's no flag per activity.

But the scoping you wanted happens anyway, because access is really governed by *assignments*, not by the flag. I verified it:

- `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments, not a catalogue
- Ivory has **0** non-RRC activities assigned
- So the only three things he can now open are the three RRC sections you gave him:
  - PT1 Section 3
  - PT21 Section 3
  - PT101 Section 2

He gets nothing else, because nothing else is assigned to him. If you later assign him a different Activity, that one becomes reachable too — that's the thing to keep in mind, not a restriction I can encode in the flag.

I left his other flags untouched: `has_practice_access`, `has_bootcamp_access`, `has_ta_access` are all still `false`.

**What he'll see now.** His three RRC rows appear on his Classroom home (they were being filtered out by exactly this flag), and the link you sent will open instead of showing "Not open yet". No action needed from him beyond a refresh — the flag is read per session, so he may need to reload the page or sign out and back in if his tab has been open since this morning.

// One definition of "a paying student's access", used by the Stripe webhook and
// anything else that needs to open the product after a payment.
//
// A new account starts locked (see the profiles column defaults): they can see
// their classroom, finish the intake, message Joshua and book. Paying turns on
// the rest. Nothing here ever revokes — a refund is Joshua's call from People,
// not something a webhook should decide.

export const PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS = [
  "has_classroom_access",
  "has_practice_access",
  "has_drill_access",
  "has_bootcamp_access",
  "has_journal_access",
  "has_waj_access",
  "has_chat_access",
  "has_schedule_access",
  "has_activity_access",
] as const;

export type GrantResult =
  | { granted: true; class_id: string }
  | { granted: false; reason: "no_email" | "no_account" };

/** Opens the paid feature set for whoever owns `email`. */
export async function grantStudentAccess(
  admin: { auth: { admin: { listUsers: (opts: { perPage: number }) => Promise<any> } }; from: (t: string) => any },
  email: string | null | undefined,
): Promise<GrantResult> {
  if (!email) return { granted: false, reason: "no_email" };
  const target = email.trim().toLowerCase();

  const { data } = await admin.auth.admin.listUsers({ perPage: 1000 });
  const user = (data?.users ?? []).find(
    (u: { email?: string | null }) => (u.email ?? "").toLowerCase() === target,
  );
  // Someone can pay before they ever make an account. That is not an error:
  // Joshua grants them by hand once they sign up, and the payment record still
  // exists either way.
  if (!user) return { granted: false, reason: "no_account" };

  const updates: Record<string, boolean> = {};
  for (const flag of PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS) updates[flag] = true;

  const { error } = await admin.from("profiles").update(updates).eq("class_id", user.id);

// -------------------------------------------------------------- resolution

interface StudentRow {
  class_id: string;
  display_name: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  [k: string]: unknown;
}

/**
 * Students come from the admin-manage-users edge function, which cross-joins
 * profiles with auth.users for the email. `class_id` is the canonical student
 * identifier everywhere in this codebase.
 */
async function listStudents(): Promise<StudentRow[]> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);
  const rows = (data ?? []) as StudentRow[];
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) throw new Error("admin-manage-users returned no user list");
  return rows;
}

/**
 * Resolve a name or email to exactly one student. Ambiguity is fatal rather
 * than resolved by picking the first: sending a student's homework to the wrong
 * account is silent — she simply never sees it, and he never learns why.
 */
function resolveStudent(rows: StudentRow[], query: string): StudentRow {
  const q = query.trim().toLowerCase();
  const exact = rows.filter(
    (r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q,
  );
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows.filter(
    (r) =>
      r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q),
  );

  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no student matches "${query}".`);
    console.error("Known:", rows.map((r) => r.display_name ?? r.email).join(" · "));
    process.exit(1);
  }

It names what is wrong and never fixes it, because opening a\n * feature on a student's account is Joshua's call.\n */\nimport fs from \"node:fs\";\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\n\nconst env: Record<string, string> = {};\nfor (const f of [path.resolve(\"../../.env\"), path.resolve(\".env\")]) {\n  if (!fs.existsSync(f)) continue;\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(f, \"utf8\").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n    const i = line.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (i > 0 && !line.startsWith(\"#\")) {\n      env[line.slice(0, i).trim()] = line.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g, \"\");\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nawait supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({\n  email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,\n  password: [REDACTED],\n});\n\n/** What each kind of assigned work needs before a student can reach it. */\nconst NEEDS = {\n  activity: \"has_activity_access\",\n  guided_drill: \"has_drill_access\",\n} as const;\n\nconst { data: assignments, error: aErr } = await supabase\n  .from(\"activity_assignments\")\n  .select(\"student_id,title,status,mode,created_at\");\nif (aErr) throw new Error(`could not read assignments: ${aErr.message}`);\n\nconst rows = (assignments ?? []) as Array<{\n  student_id: string;\n  title: string;\n  status: string;\n  mode: string | null;\n  created_at: string;\n}>;\n\nconst ids = [...new Set(rows.map((r) => r.student_id))].filter(Boolean);\nconst { data: profiles, error: pErr } = await supabase\n  .from(\"profiles\")\n  .select(\"class_id,display_name,has_activity_access,has_drill_access\")\n  .in(\"class_id\", ids);\nif (pErr) throw new Error(`could not read profiles: ${pErr.message}`);\n\nconst byId = new Map(\n  ((profiles ?? []) as Array<Record<string, unknown>>).map((p) => [p.class_id as string, p]),\n);\n\nlet blocked = 0;\nfor (const id of ids) {\n  const profile = byId.get(id);\n  const mine = rows.filter((r) => r.student_id === id);\n  const who = (profile?.display_name as string) ?? `(no profile for ${id.slice(0, 8)})`;\n\n  if (!profile) {\n    console.log(`MISSING PROFILE  ${who}  ${mine.length} assignment(s)`);\n    blocked++;\n    continue;\n  }\n  const flag = NEEDS.activity;\n  if (profile[flag] === false) {\n    blocked++;\n    const open = mine.filter((r) => r.status !== \"completed\");\n    console.log(\n      `BLOCKED  ${who.padEnd(24)} ${flag}=false  ${mine.length} assigned, ${open.length} still open`,\n    );\n    for (const r of open.slice(0, 5)) {\n      console.log(`         \"${r.title}\"  ${r.created_at?.slice(0, 10)}`);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nconsole.log(\n  blocked === 0\n    ? `\\nClean. ${ids.length} students with assigned work, all of them able to open it.`\n    : `\\n${blocked} of ${ids.length} students cannot open work that was assigned to them.`,\n);\n"}

## assistant

All four done.

## The reply

Drafted in Gmail, in his existing thread, correctly threaded with `In-Reply-To`. **Not sent** — it's sitting as a draft for you to review. Copy also in the vault.

It opens by owning it in the first line, names the cause without jargon, tells him the three sections by name, and flags the sign-out-and-back-in in case his browser is holding stale permissions. 195 words, no em-dashes, no banned phrases.

## It works

Verified rather than assumed:
- `has_activity_access` read back as `true` on his profile
- `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments, and he has **0** non-RRC activities, so the flag opened exactly the three RRC sections and nothing else
- His other flags (`practice`, `bootcamp`, `ta`) are untouched and still `false`

## It can't happen to anyone else

Two layers, pushed as `15a5a2b`:

**`scripts/assign.ts` now grants the flag the assigned work needs** — after the write, never before, since a student who's never assigned anything has no business having a feature opened. It grants nothing else, revokes nothing, and fails loudly rather than reporting a clean assign.

**`scripts/access-audit.ts`** is the read-only sweep for work arriving by other paths — admin UI, Lovable-side writes, hand-run SQL. It names problems without fixing them, because opening a feature on a student's account is your call. Ran it: **clean, 13 students with assigned work, all able to open it.**

## Logged, and what I'd do differently

Vault entry in `wiki/log.md`, plus a memory so it carries into future sessions.

The lesson worth keeping: **the product had no opinion about whether a student could open what it had just written to their account.** Every route is gated and every feed filters on the same flag, so a missing flag isn't a locked door — it's an empty page. The failure is silent on both sides: you see a successful assign, he sees nothing. The only detector was a student polite enough to send a screenshot.

Where I'd have done better: I've spent this session on the Report Card's *contents* while the path to it was the thing actually breaking for students. Celia couldn't find her card because a button looked like a caption; Ivory couldn't open his drill because of a boolean. Both were reachability, not design. Worth checking the route before polishing what's at the end of it.

=== README ===
---
title: Students — one folder per student
type: operating-layer
tags: [students, operating-layer]
created: 2026-07-03

status: active
subject: Students - one folder per student
aliases: []

# Students

One folder per student, holding **everything** about them (D2, locked 2026-07-16): transcripts, correspondence, homework, notes, schedules, profile. Open the student's folder and their whole working record is there. This is one of the brain's three knowledge systems, beside [[../wiki/index|wiki]] and [[../corpus/index|corpus]].

## Current students

- [[bella-lorenzo/bella-lorenzo|Bella Lorenzo]] · [[c-alexander/c-alexander|C. Alexander]] · [[dakota/dakota|Dakota]] · [[david-salgado/david-salgado|David Salgado]] · [[ethan/ethan|Ethan]] · [[favour/favour|Favour]] · [[lexi/lexi|Lexi A]] · [[lydia/lydia|Lydia]] · [[meghan/meghan|Meghan]] · [[nicole/nicole|Nicole]] · [[sofia/sofia|Sofia]] · [[yahunatan-zetvy/yahunatan-zetvy|Yahunatan Zetvy]]

## The primary/mirror boundary (D4 — read before regenerating anything)

Every file in a student folder is one of two kinds, and the distinction decides what a rebuild may overwrite:

- **Primary — the brain is the only home.** Transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, study plans, anything Joshua or an agent authored about the student. **Never regenerate, never overwrite; retain forever.**
- **Mirror — a projection of a live backend.** Session/booking state (Cal.com + Google Calendar), homework/dossier/publish state (LSAT U Supabase), payment events (Stripe), correspondence indexes (Gmail). On any disagreement the backend wins, silently.

**Convention:** mirror files MUST be named `*.generated.md` (or carry `generated: true` frontmatter). A file without that marker is primary by definition and must be treated as precious. Pre-2026-07-16 files (`email-context.md`, `payment-links.md`, `transcripts.md`) predate the marker — treat as primary until individually re-derived and renamed. No Markdown file here is ever the source of operational truth — no package balances, no running totals (all services are paid upfront; Cal.com answers sessions-used).

## Standard files per student folder

- `<student-slug>.md` — compact student index and routing page (primary).
- `email-context.md` — synthesized Gmail context.
- `email-correspondence.md` — Gmail message-id corpus for the student's confirmed address.
- `payment-links.md` — verified payment, billing, and scheduling links.
- `transcripts.md` — transcript and meeting-note inventory.
- `teaching-notes.md` — durable, evidence-backed learning judgments and teaching patterns (primary).
- `open-loops.md` — unfinished follow-ups and pending decisions (primary).
- `live-status.generated.md` — refreshable operational summary from Calendar, Gmail, payments, and LSAT U (generated mirror).
- `transcript-downloads/` — full local transcript exports (primary — the brain is their only home).
- `sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-session-name/` — new self-contained session packages.

=== _templates ===
students/_templates:
session

students/_templates/session:
summary.md
topic-map.md
transcript.md

# Student name aliases

Maps the names that show up in transcript filenames, calendar invites, and email
to the canonical student folder slug in `students/`.

It exists because Gemini names its exports from the meeting title, which is
whatever was typed at booking time: `Sofia`, `sofia`, `Sofia Minassian`,
`LSAT Session between Joshua and Meghan Mullins` all mean one student. Without
this file every pass re-derives the same mapping by hand and gets it slightly
differently.

Read by `system/scripts/transcript-inventory.py`. Format is one bullet per
alias, `alias -> folder-slug`. Matching is case- and punctuation-insensitive,
so only add entries the automatic matcher cannot get on its own (exact slug,
first name, and unambiguous containment are already handled).

## Aliases

- Adriana -> adriana-varea
- Adriana Varea -> adriana-varea

- Yahunatan -> yahunatan-zetvy

{"command": "export PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8; python - <<'PY' > /tmp/digests.md 2>/tmp/dig.err\nimport glob,os,re\nNEW=[\"celia-cutler\",\"steven-morris\",\"vinay-kalva\",\"asia\",\"fernande-charleus\",\"whit-wilson\",\"charles-moore\"]\nRECENT={\"adriana-varea\":[\"2026-08\",\"2026-09\"],\"yahunatan-zetvy\":[\"2026-08\",\"2026-09\"],\"sofia\":[\"2026-08\",\"2026-09\"]}\ndef digest(path):\n    t=open(path,encoding='utf-8',errors='replace').read()\n    cut=t.lower().find(\"\\ntranscript\")\n    head=t[:cut] if cut>0 else t[:9000]\n    lines=[l.rstrip() for l in head.split(\"\\n\")]\n    # topic headings: a non-bullet line whose next non-empty line is a bullet\n    topics=[]\n    for i,l in enumerate(lines):\n        s=l.strip()\n        if not s or s.startswith((\"*\",\"•\",\"-\")) or \"@\" in s or len(s)>80: continue\n        nxt=next((x.strip() for x in lines[i+1:i+3] if x.strip()), \"\")\n        if nxt.startswith((\"*\",\"•\")) and s.lower() not in (\"attachments\",\"meeting records\",\"quick notes\"):\n            topics.append(s)\n    # summary: longest plain sentence before the first topic\n    stop=lines.index(topics[0]) if topics else len(lines)\n    cands=[l.strip() for l in lines[:stop] if l.strip().endswith(\".\") and len(l.strip())>60]\n    summ=max(cands,key=len) if cands else \"\"\n    return summ, topics\nfor slug in NEW+list(RECENT):\n    files=sorted(glob.glob(f\"students/{slug}/transcript-downloads/*.md\"))\n    if slug in RECENT: files=[f for f in files if any(p in os.path.basename(f) for p in RECENT[slug])]\n    print(f\"\\n### {slug}  ({len(files)} sessions)\")\n    for f in files:\n        s,tp=digest(f)\n        print(f\"- **{os.path.basename(f)[:10]}** {s[:200] if s else '(no summary line)'}\")\n        if tp: print(f\"    - {' | '.join(tp[:7])[:250]}\")\nPY\nhead -c 2600 /tmp/digests.md", "description": "Re-extract digests with better parsing"}

Your README is explicit that anything without a `.generated.md` marker is primary and precious, so I didn't fabricate into those slots. `email-context.md`, `email-correspondence.md` and `payment-links.md` are mirrors of Gmail and Stripe and want their own pass. `teaching-notes.md` needs a read of the full transcripts, not the summary lines — that's the natural next job, and it's a big one: about 1.5MB of transcript.
